C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

人道主义环境中应对 疫苗 可预防疾病 暴发 的机遇、挑战和建议解决方案

Opportunities, Challenges and Proposed Solutions in Responding to Vaccine Preventable Disease Outbreaks in Humanitarian Settings
作者： Kemish Kenneth Alier, Yirong Chew, William J. Moss, Paul Spiegel, Chiara Altare
摘要中文翻译人道主义危机日益扰乱免疫系统,加速 传播 可预防疾病 (VPD) 的发生。尽管有强大的全球响应架构,但人道主义环境中的反应性 疫苗接种 活动仍然缺乏记录、经常延迟且操作受到限制。我们对 2015 年至 2024 年间人道主义环境中的已发表文献和灰色文献进行了结构化搜索,以识别和描述针对 5 个 VPD(包括 麻疹、脊髓灰质炎、白喉、脑膜炎球菌性脑膜炎和黄热病)的反应性 疫苗接种 运动,利用了 316 个已发表文献和灰色文献来源,包括 WHO 疾病 麻疹 News、GOARN 和 ReliefWeb。鉴于公开报告中存在巨大差距,调查结果作为记录实施差距和吸取实际教训的角度提出,而不是作为系统审查。在 132 个已识别的 暴发 中,有 83 个 (63%) 记录了反应性 疫苗接种 活动。据报道,中位反应滞后为 96 天(范围:14-533)。可获得 26 个 暴发 (20%) 的覆盖率数据,范围从 3.7% 到 109%。实施障碍包括政治和安全限制、治理和协调失败、社区犹豫和不信任以及运营和技术故障。记录的创新包括实时数字监控、外交谈判访问、社区主导的交付和自适应多机构协调。尽管存在全球 疫苗接种 机制,但在文档、及时性和覆盖范围方面仍然存在严重差距。缩小这些差距需要标准化的最低限度报告、具有明确决策权的预先建立的协调框架、嵌入式社区参与以及针对不同人道主义背景量身定制的具体操作指南。系统化并资助经过实地验证的解决方案对于保护最弱势群体和加强全球卫生安全至关重要。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本次审查重点关注人道主义环境中应对 暴发 和 疫苗 可预防疾病 (VPD) 的挑战和机遇,特别强调 麻疹病毒。核心问题是如何改进这些背景下反应性 疫苗接种 活动的记录、及时性和覆盖范围。
证据基础与方法
该综述基于对已发表文献和灰色文献(包括世界卫生组织疾病 暴发 新闻、GOARN 和 ReliefWeb)的结构化搜索,旨在识别和描述 2015 年至 2024 年间跨人道主义环境中五个 VPD(包括 麻疹)的反应性 疫苗接种 活动。总共分析了 316 个来源,但由于公开报告中的空白,该综述并未作为系统综述呈现。
共识与结论
在人道主义环境中,麻疹 和其他 VPD 的反应性 疫苗接种 活动经常被延迟且操作受到限制。中位反应滞后为 96 天(范围:14-533),覆盖率数据仅适用于 20% 的 暴发,范围为 3.7% 至 109%。这些发现凸显了改进文档和运营策略的必要性。
争议与缺口
公开的报告存在巨大差距,这限制了得出全面结论的能力。该综述以视角而非系统综述的形式呈现研究结果,表明证据基础不足以进行正式的荟萃分析。
科研与实践启示
为了弥补这些差距,审查建议需要标准化的最低限度报告、具有明确决策权的预先建立的协调框架、嵌入式社区参与以及针对不同人道主义背景的具体情况行动指南。系统化并资助经过实地验证的解决方案对于保护弱势群体和加强全球卫生安全至关重要。

Opportunities, Challenges and Proposed Solutions in Responding to Vaccine Preventable Disease Outbreaks in Humanitarian Settings

Authors: Kemish Kenneth Alier, Yirong Chew, William J. Moss, Paul Spiegel, Chiara Altare
Original abstract (English)
Humanitarian crises increasingly disrupt immunization systems, accelerating transmission of vaccine-preventable diseases (VPDs). Despite a robust global response architecture, reactive vaccination campaigns in humanitarian settings remain poorly documented, frequently delayed, and operationally constrained. We conducted a structured search of published and grey literature to identify and describe reactive vaccination campaigns for five VPDs, including measles, poliomyelitis, diphtheria, meningococcal meningitis, and yellow fever, across humanitarian settings between 2015 and 2024, drawing on 316 published and grey literature sources including WHO Disease Outbreak News, GOARN, and ReliefWeb. Given substantial gaps in publicly available reporting, findings are presented as a perspective to document implementation gaps and draw practical lessons rather than as a systematic review. Of 132 identified outbreaks, reactive vaccination campaigns were documented in 83 (63%). Where reported, the median response lag was 96 days (range: 14–533). Coverage data were available for 26 outbreaks (20%), ranging from 3.7% to 109%. Implementation barriers include political and security constraints, governance and coordination failures, community hesitancy and mistrust, and operational and technical breakdowns. Documented innovations included real-time digital monitoring, diplomatically negotiated access, community-led delivery, and adaptive multi-agency coordination. Despite existing global vaccination mechanisms, critical gaps in documentation, timeliness, and coverage persist. Closing these gaps requires standardized minimum reporting, pre-established coordination frameworks with defined decision authority, embedded community engagement, and context-specific operational guidance tailored to distinct humanitarian contexts. Systematizing and funding field-proven solutions is essential to protect the most vulnerable populations and strengthen global health security.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the challenges and opportunities in responding to outbreaks of vaccine-preventable diseases (VPDs) in humanitarian settings, with a particular emphasis on measles virus. The central question is how to improve the documentation, timeliness, and coverage of reactive vaccination campaigns in these contexts.
Evidence base and method
The review is based on a structured search of published and grey literature, including WHO Disease Outbreak News, GOARN, and ReliefWeb, to identify and describe reactive vaccination campaigns for five VPDs, including measles, across humanitarian settings between 2015 and 2024. A total of 316 sources were analyzed, but the review is not presented as a systematic review due to gaps in publicly available reporting.
Consensus and conclusions
Reactive vaccination campaigns for measles and other VPDs in humanitarian settings are frequently delayed and operationally constrained. The median response lag was 96 days (range: 14–533), and coverage data were available for only 20% of outbreaks, ranging from 3.7% to 109%. These findings highlight the need for improved documentation and operational strategies.
Controversies and gaps
There are substantial gaps in publicly available reporting, which limits the ability to draw comprehensive conclusions. The review presents findings as a perspective rather than a systematic review, indicating that the evidence base is not robust enough for a formal meta-analysis.
Research and practice implications
To address these gaps, the review suggests the need for standardized minimum reporting, pre-established coordination frameworks with defined decision authority, embedded community engagement, and context-specific operational guidance tailored to distinct humanitarian contexts. Systematizing and funding field-proven solutions is essential to protect vulnerable populations and strengthen global health security.

废水 监测 扩大到包括多种病原体,其中包括 麻疹病毒 - 美国公共卫生协会

Wastewater surveillance expands to include multiple pathogens, including measles virus - American Public Health Association
新闻简报美国公共卫生协会报告称,麻疹病毒 的废水 麻疹病毒 已在美国扩大,包括在多个州进行测试。这种做法始于 COVID-19 大流行期间,现在用于检测包括 麻疹 在内的一系列病原体,以指导公共卫生应对措施。在北卡罗来纳州,2025 年 6 月增加了 麻疹 检测,并在 10 个县记录了 4 次废水检测以及 24 例记录在案的病例。官员们发现阳性检测与 麻疹 病例之间存在相关性,四分之三的检测发生在症状出现后四天内。在没有报告病例的情况下进行的检测促使开设了社区 疫苗接种 诊所。然而,在农村地区,废水监测的效果有限,那里许多居民使用私人化粪池,无法监测病原体。这种做法被视为补充案例 监测 和预防 暴发 的宝贵工具,并计划在未来扩大监控范围。
查看新闻五要素
时间
麻疹病毒 的废水 麻疹 在美国已扩大,在 COVID-19 大流行期间增加了检测,并在 2026 年持续监测。在北卡罗来纳州,于 2025 年 6 月增加了 麻疹 检测,此后一直进行检测。在加利福尼亚州,测试今年一直在进行。一次未报告病例的检测促使 疫苗接种 社区诊所于 2026 年开业。
地点与对象
美国,特别提到加利福尼亚州(39 个县的 89 个站点)和北卡罗来纳州(29 个县的 36 个站点)。人口包括这些州的社区和个人,值得注意的是,在北卡罗来纳州,只有大约一半的人口使用下水道系统,其余的则使用私人化粪池。
事件
麻疹病毒 的废水处理 麻疹病毒 已在美国扩大,包括在多个州进行测试。公共卫生官员发现废水检测呈阳性与 麻疹 病例之间存在相关性,这种做法正被用来指导公共卫生应对措施。
规模、影响与风险
美国已报告超过 4,800 起 麻疹 病例。北卡罗来纳州已在 10 个县记录了 24 起 麻疹 病例,并发现了 4 起废水 麻疹 病例。废水检测与 麻疹 病例之间存在相关性,四分之三的检测发生在症状出现后四天内。该做法被用来补充案例 监测 并指导公共卫生行动。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生官员正在测试废水中的 麻疹病毒 和其他病原体,以预防或减轻 麻疹病毒。在北卡罗来纳州,官员们在受感染地区创建了广泛的通信信息计划。然而,农村地区私人化粪池缺乏监测限制了废水监测的有效性。科罗拉多州的试点计划计划扩大社区监测和医院废水采样。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Wastewater surveillance expands to include multiple pathogens, including measles virus - American Public Health Association

News Brief
The American Public Health Association reports that wastewater surveillance for measles virus has expanded in the U.S., including testing in multiple states. This practice, which began during the COVID-19 pandemic, is now used to detect a range of pathogens, including measles, to guide public health responses. In North Carolina, testing for measles was added in June 2025, and four wastewater detections have been recorded alongside 24 documented cases in 10 counties. Officials have found a correlation between positive tests and measles cases, with three-quarters of detections occurring within four days of symptom onset. A detection without a reported case prompted the opening of a community vaccination clinic. However, the effectiveness of wastewater surveillance is limited in rural areas where many residents use private septic tanks, which cannot be monitored for pathogens. The practice is seen as a valuable tool to complement case surveillance and prevent outbreaks, with plans to expand monitoring in the future.
Original source text (English)
Public Health Newswire from The Nation’s Health brings you the news you need now Public Health Newswire Wastewater surveillance expands to include multiple pathogens, including measles virus November 4, 2026 Mark Barna Wastewater monitoring to jump-start prevention of infectious outbreaks has expanded in the U.S. in recent years. At a Tuesday session, “ Wastewater-based Epidemiology: Advances and Future Direction in Wastewater Surveillance ,” experts discussed how the practice has grown into a surveillance juggernaut, in which scientists test for multiple pathogens, including the measles virus, to prompt timely public health action. U.S. surveillance increased during the COVID-19 pandemic as wastewater testing ramped up for SARS-CoV-2. These days, public health officials are testing locally for a long list of viruses to prevent or mitigate outbreaks. Among them are RSV, influenza, norovirus, mpox, foodborne illnesses and even antimicrobial resistance pathogens. As of Nov. 1, over 4,800 cases of measles have been reported in the U.S. this year, and more states are monitoring wastewater for the measles virus. California, which this year has had 59 measles cases, tests wastewater for the pathogen at 89 sites in 39 counties. Officials have found a correlation between positive tests and measles cases. “Wastewater surveillance may be effective in identifying presence of measles, complementing case surveillance and guiding public health response,” said Mariah Otstot of the California Department of Public Health. North Carolina monitors wastewater for five pathogens at 36 sites in 29 counties. The state added measles testing in June 2025. It has had four wastewater measles detections, along with 24 documented measles cases in 10 counties, according to data at the North Carolina Department of Health . A correlation in test sites and measles infections has been found. Department workers have created extensive communication messaging programs in infected areas. Three-quarters of wastewater detections occurred within four days of the symptom onset in communities. A detection without a reported case prompted the opening of a community vaccination clinic. Because North Carolina is mostly rural, only about half the population is on sewer systems. The rest use private septic tanks as their primary household wastewater treatment. That impedes pathogen detection in wastewater because there is no current way to monitor private septic tanks for pathogens, said Nicole Snyder, an epidemiologist at the North Carolina Department of Health and Human Services. Snyder was not confident things would change in the conservative state. “Different people are more receptive or less receptive to” a government employee surveilling homeowner septic tanks, Snyder said. Antimicrobial resistance — when bacteria and viruses adapt effective shields against antibiotics and antivirals — is an urgent public health threat. Nearly 51,000 people die in the U.S. each year because of resistance, according ot the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. A chief culprit are resistant microbes in livestock finding their way into humans through the food supply. Colorado has begun a pilot program that monitors wastewater at nine sites for resistant pathogens. Beginning in 2025, the Colorado Department of Public Health and Environment tested about 2,000 wastewater samples, finding significant correlation between wastewater detections and prevalence of antimicrobial resistance in a region. A second correlation involved finding specific resistant pathogens in regions where the relevant livestock is raised. For example, OXA-48-like enzymes, which have been connected to antimicrobial resistance and found in hogs, were detected in regions where hog farming is common. “OXA-48-like had the greatest proportion of samples, increasing in eight regions,” said Eve Curran, a health professional at the department. The pilot program is planning to generate additional community monitoring and hospital wastewater sampling, Curran said. Photo by Onuma Inthapong, courtesy iStockphoto. Explore more topics APHA 2026 Annual Meeting Community Health Infectious Disease
View five news elements
Time
Wastewater surveillance for measles virus has expanded in the U.S., with increased testing during the COVID-19 pandemic and ongoing monitoring in 2026. In North Carolina, measles testing was added in June 2025, and detections have occurred since then. In California, testing has been ongoing this year. A detection without a reported case prompted the opening of a community vaccination clinic in 2026.
Location and population
The U.S., with specific mention of California (89 sites in 39 counties) and North Carolina (36 sites in 29 counties). The population includes communities and individuals in these states, with a note that in North Carolina, only about half the population is on sewer systems, while the rest use private septic tanks.
Event
Wastewater surveillance for measles virus has expanded in the U.S., including testing in multiple states. Public health officials have found a correlation between positive wastewater tests and measles cases, and the practice is being used to guide public health responses.
Scale, impact and risk
Over 4,800 measles cases have been reported in the U.S. North Carolina has documented 24 measles cases in 10 counties and detected four wastewater measles cases. A correlation between wastewater detections and measles cases has been found, with three-quarters of detections occurring within four days of symptom onset. The practice is being used to complement case surveillance and guide public health action.
Response, status and uncertainty
Public health officials are testing wastewater for measles virus and other pathogens to prevent or mitigate outbreaks. In North Carolina, officials have created extensive communication messaging programs in infected areas. However, the lack of monitoring for private septic tanks in rural areas limits the effectiveness of wastewater surveillance. The pilot program in Colorado is planning to expand community monitoring and hospital wastewater sampling.

Wisconsin reports first measles death; Pennsylvania announces 6th as cases climb nationally - American Hospital Association

News Brief
The American Hospital Association reported on November 6, 2026, that Wisconsin has recorded its first measles-related death, while Pennsylvania has announced its sixth case of measles. This comes as measles cases are rising nationally. The report highlights the severity of the situation but does not provide specific details on the investigation, testing, or response measures taken. The evidence does not mention any explicit risk level or further actions being implemented. The information is based on official statements from the AHA, which serves as the source of the report.

“非常不寻常”:宾夕法尼亚州 麻疹 病例中三分之二是成年人,带来了独特的挑战 - CIDRAP

‘Quite unusual’: Adults make up two-thirds of measles cases in Pennsylvania, presenting unique challenges - CIDRAP
新闻简报最近的一份报告强调,在宾夕法尼亚州,成年人占 麻疹 病例的三分之二,这给 麻疹 的管理带来了独特的挑战。CDC 已确认今年美国有 4 例与 麻疹 相关的死亡,而目前已报告至少 8 例。宾夕法尼亚州仍然是 麻疹 活动的中心,有 1,467 例病例。暴发 的原因是大量未接种疫苗的人群和群体免疫力减弱。公共卫生官员正在调查该病毒的传播情况,并强调需要改进 疫苗 的接受度和准备情况。这种情况凸显了解决 疫苗 犹豫和加强社区免疫力以防止进一步并发症和死亡的重要性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

‘Quite unusual’: Adults make up two-thirds of measles cases in Pennsylvania, presenting unique challenges - CIDRAP

News Brief
A recent report highlights that adults account for two-thirds of measles cases in Pennsylvania, presenting unique challenges in outbreak management. The CDC has confirmed 4 measles-related deaths in the US this year, while at least 8 have been reported. Pennsylvania remains the epicenter of measles activity with 1,467 cases. The outbreak is attributed to a large unvaccinated population and waning herd immunity. Public health officials are investigating the spread and emphasizing the need for improved vaccine acceptance and preparedness. The situation underscores the importance of addressing vaccine hesitancy and strengthening community immunity to prevent further complications and deaths.

家长需要了解的 麻疹 暴发 遍布美国

What parents need to know as measles outbreaks spread across the US
新闻简报随着 麻疹 暴发 在美国蔓延,卫生官员报告称,45 个州和华盛顿特区有近 5,200 例病例,其中 8 人死亡,其中包括威斯康星州的一名婴儿。这标志着该州自 1990 年以来首例与 麻疹 相关的死亡。CDC 强调,麻疹 具有高度传染性,每个感染者都可能将病毒传播给 18 名未接种疫苗的人。症状包括皮疹、咳嗽、发烧和科普利克斑,并发症可能包括耳部感染、严重肺炎、脑膜炎,甚至死亡。CDC 和专家强调 MMR 疫苗 对于预防的重要性,并指出其安全性和有效性。然而,某些群体,例如患有癌症的儿童或孕妇,可能不符合 疫苗接种 的资格,这强调了医疗咨询的必要性。目前情况仍在调查中,暴发 没有放缓的迹象。

2026 年 11 月 6 日下午 5:24。
地点与对象
美国 45 个州和华盛顿特区未接种疫苗的个人。

麻疹 麻疹 已蔓延至美国各地,据报道有 8 人死亡,其中包括威斯康星州的一名婴儿,这是该州自 1990 年以来首例与 麻疹 相关的死亡事件。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

What parents need to know as measles outbreaks spread across the US

News Brief
As measles outbreaks spread across the U.S., health officials report nearly 5,200 cases in 45 states and Washington, D.C., with eight deaths, including an infant in Wisconsin. This marks the first measles-related death in the state since 1990. The CDC highlights that measles is highly contagious, with each infected person potentially spreading the virus to 18 unvaccinated individuals. Symptoms include a rash, cough, fever, and Koplik spots, while complications can range from ear infections to severe pneumonias, meningitis, and even death. The CDC and experts stress the importance of the MMR vaccine for prevention, noting its safety and effectiveness. However, some groups, such as children with cancer or pregnant women, may not be eligible for vaccination, underscoring the need for medical consultation. The situation remains under investigation, with no signs of the outbreaks slowing down.
Original source text (English)
ABC News Live Video Local News Shows Good Morning America ShopGMA Interest Successfully Added We'll notify you here with news about Turn on desktop notifications for breaking stories about interest? OffOn Stream on Wellness What parents need to know as measles outbreaks spread across the US New measles death in Wisconsin CDC ByYi-Jin Yu November 6, 2026, 5:24 PM More measles cases are being reported across the U.S., and eight people have died so far, including an infant in Wisconsin, the first measles-related death in the state since 1990, according to health officials. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention says there have been nearly 5,200 measles cases across 45 states and Washington, D.C., so far this year, as more have decided to opt out of vaccination. "I'm extremely concerned, and the reason for that is there's no indication that these outbreaks will be slowing down at this point," Dr. Andrew Handel, a pediatric infectious diseases physician at Stony Brook Children's Hospital in Stony Brook, New York, told ABC News. As the measles virus continues to spread, here's what parents should know about the disease. What is measles? Measles is a highly contagious virus that can cause acute respiratory illness, according to the CDC. It is more contagious that other respiratory viruses, such as the common cold, the flu or COVID. "It is the most contagious infection that we know of," Handel said. "The estimate is that for every person who has measles, they can infect up to 18 people who are unvaccinated with it." The measles virus in an image from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention website. CDC Measles is spread through the air when someone infected with the virus exhales or coughs. People can also become infected after breathing in contaminated air or by touching an infected surface and then touching their eyes, noses or mouths, the CDC states. "For measles, it spreads through airborne means, which means that once [an infected person breathes] it out, it actually stays circulating in the air for up to two hours after they leave that room," Handel explained. "If someone goes to their doctor to be checked for measles, and they don't recognize that they have measles, and then they walk out of the room, someone who comes in an hour and a half later can become infected with measles." What are the symptoms of measles? The CDC notes that measles can initially look like other respiratory infections, with symptoms including a cough, runny nose, conjunctivitis (red, watery eyes), or a high fever, which can increase to more than 104 degrees Fahrenheit. The most recognizable symptom of measles is a rash that typically starts at the head and moves down the body. "People break out into the rash, which starts as red, dark circles that usually start right at the hairline and work their way down the body," Handel said. "As it works its way down, you see the red circles, the red dots further down. But the place where it started -- so, on the face -- the redness will start to come together, so it'll look like a sheet of redness, and it, again, starts from the head and works its way down, which is very particular." Related Measles cases surpass 5,000 in the US with infections confirmed in 45 states: CDC "We also see something called Koplik spots, which are usually whitish, bluish dots that can appear on the inside of the cheek," Handel added. "Those tend to come up a couple days before the rash, so it can help us to make the diagnosis a bit earlier." Popular Reads What to know about recounts in Mississippi with razor-thin margin in Senate race 2 days ago Kimmarie Johnson, 'Beverly Hills, 90210' actress, dies after heart emergency at 50 1 day ago Captain identified after deadly Florida Learjet crash Nov 3 Measles symptoms typically begin seven to 14 days after initial contact with the virus, according to the CDC. What are the complications of measles? A common misconception about measles, as the CDC notes, is that infection with the virus isn't severe. The CDC and Handel both stress that measles infections can give rise to serious complications, from short-term issues like diarrhea and ear infections, to serious to life-threatening long-term problems and death. "Many children, many people can go on and have complications ... that can include severe pneumonias, dehydration, and also acute brain infections, where suddenly, you can have seizures," Handel said. He continued, "You can have changes in behavior. You can develop meningitis where there's neck pain and headache because the measles infection [has] infiltrated into the spinal fluid. There are some children who, especially if they're infected before they turn 1 year old, in particular, can develop a neurologic progressive disease [called subacute sclerosing panencephalitis, or SSPE,] that ultimately results in death up to 10 years later. And so, there are some really scary outcomes from it." Related As measles continues to spread, health officials are seeing vaccine interest rise How can measles be prevented? According to the CDC, the best way to protect against measles is with the measles, mumps, and rubella vaccine, or MMR, which can be administered to children starting at 12 months old. Children who receive a first dose of the MMR vaccine then need to get a second dose when they are between 4 and 6 years old. "The only way to prevent infections when there is an ongoing national outbreak at levels that we haven't seen in decades is to get the MMR vaccine," Handel said. "It is safe, it is effective, and it's the best thing we can do again to stop this outbreak and get the country back to the rates of measles infections that we'd previously seen." Not everyone may be able to get an MMR vaccine, such as children with cancer, those who are pregnant, and people who have compromised immune systems, so it is important to discuss your children's and family's eligibility and concerns with a medical provider. November 6, 2026, 5:24 PM.
Location and population
United States, 45 states and Washington, D.C., unvaccinated individuals.
Event
Measles outbreaks have spread across the U.S., with eight reported deaths, including an infant in Wisconsin, the first measles-related death in the state since 1990.
Scale, impact and risk
Nearly 5,200 measles cases have been reported this year, with the potential for each infected person to spread the virus to up to 18 unvaccinated individuals. Complications include severe pneumonias, dehydration, acute brain infections, seizures, and long-term neurologic disease leading to death.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health officials and the CDC are monitoring the outbreaks, emphasizing the importance of the MMR vaccine for prevention. The CDC states that measles is the most contagious infection known, and the outbreaks are ongoing with no indication of slowing down.

一名儿童死于 麻疹,这是威斯康星州自 1990 年以来首例此类死亡事件 - STAT

Child dies of measles in Wisconsin’s first such death since 1990 - STAT
新闻简报据 STAT 报道,威斯康星州一名儿童死于 麻疹,这是该州自 1990 年以来首次出现此类死亡。该事件凸显了人们对 疫苗 覆盖率下降和该疾病可能卷土重来的担忧。威斯康星州卫生部门正在调查此案,但没有提及具体的遏制措施或风险级别。这一事件强调了维持高 疫苗接种 率对于预防 暴发 和保护弱势群体的重要性。这一死亡事件提醒人们,麻疹 相关的持续公共卫生挑战,特别是在免疫率较低的地区。报告强调需要持续保持警惕和教育,以确保社区免疫力。

事件
一名儿童死于 麻疹,这是威斯康星州自 1990 年以来首次出现此类死亡事件。STAT 报道了这一事件。
规模、影响与风险
已确认 1 人死亡。威斯康星州的 麻疹 暴发 引起了对 疫苗 覆盖范围和公共卫生准备的担忧。
应对、状态与不确定性
威斯康星州卫生部门正在调查此案。证据中没有提及具体的遏制措施或风险级别。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Child dies of measles in Wisconsin’s first such death since 1990 - STAT

News Brief
A child in Wisconsin has died from measles, the first such death in the state since 1990, according to a report by STAT. The incident highlights concerns about declining vaccine coverage and the potential resurgence of the disease. The Wisconsin health department is investigating the case, but no specific containment measures or risk levels have been mentioned. This event underscores the importance of maintaining high vaccination rates to prevent outbreaks and protect vulnerable populations. The death serves as a reminder of the ongoing public health challenges related to measles, particularly in areas with lower immunization rates. The report emphasizes the need for continued vigilance and education to ensure community immunity.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

宾夕法尼亚州的 麻疹 病例超过 1,000 例,这是该州数十年来最大的 麻疹 - ABC News - 突发新闻、最新新闻和视频

Measles cases surpass 1,000 in Pennsylvania amid the state's largest outbreak in decades - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos
新闻简报截至 2026 年 11 月 6 日,宾夕法尼亚州正在经历数十年来最大规模的 麻疹 暴发,已报告病例超过 1,000 例。美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)证实了这一令人担忧的趋势,凸显了情况的严重性。麻疹 引起了对公共卫生和 疫苗接种 发病率的担忧,因为 麻疹 是一种高度传染性的病毒性疾病,可能导致严重的并发症。当局正在通过监测和实施预防措施来遏制疫情蔓延。CDC 的报告强调需要加强 疫苗接种 的努力和公众意识,以减轻 暴发 的影响。这一事件是 麻疹 在美国复苏的更广泛趋势的一部分,强调了保持高免疫覆盖率以防止进一步传播的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
ABC新闻直播 当地新闻节目 《早安美国》 ShopGMA 感兴趣成功添加 我们将在此处通知您有关 开启桌面通知以获取您感兴趣的突发新闻?
地点与对象
美国宾夕法尼亚州。
事件
据 CDC 报告,宾夕法尼亚州的 麻疹 病例超过 1,000 例,这是该州数十年来最大的 麻疹 病例。
规模、影响与风险
宾夕法尼亚州已报告超过 1,000 起 麻疹 病例,表明存在重大 麻疹 病例。CDC 已确认这是该州数十年来最大的 麻疹 暴发。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 正在积极监测 暴发,公共卫生官员正在实施控制措施。目前情况仍在调查中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles cases surpass 1,000 in Pennsylvania amid the state's largest outbreak in decades - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

News Brief
As of November 6, 2026, Pennsylvania is experiencing its largest measles outbreak in decades, with over 1,000 cases reported. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) confirmed this alarming trend, highlighting the severity of the situation. The outbreak has raised concerns about public health and vaccination rates, as measles is a highly contagious viral disease that can lead to serious complications. Authorities are working to contain the spread through monitoring and implementing preventive measures. The CDC's report underscores the need for increased vaccination efforts and public awareness to mitigate the impact of the outbreak. This event is part of a broader trend of measles resurgence in the United States, emphasizing the importance of maintaining high immunization coverage to prevent further spread.
Original source text (English)

Measles cases surpass 1,000 in Pennsylvania amid the state's largest outbreak in decades, reported by the CDC.
Scale, impact and risk
Over 1,000 measles cases have been reported in Pennsylvania, indicating a significant outbreak. The CDC has confirmed this as the state's largest measles outbreak in decades.
Response, status and uncertainty
The CDC is actively monitoring the outbreak, and public health officials are implementing measures to contain it. The situation is ongoing and under investigation.

威斯康星州西南部婴儿的死亡与 麻疹 暴发 有关

Infant’s death linked to measles outbreak in southwest Wisconsin
新闻简报一名婴儿的死亡与威斯康星州西南部的 麻疹 麻疹 有关,这是该州 35 年来首例与 麻疹 相关的死亡事件。暴发 已影响四个县的近 400 人,其原因是 疫苗接种 发病率较低,超过 99% 的病例涉及未接种疫苗的人。威斯康星州卫生服务部 (DHS) 确认了这起死亡事件,并将 麻疹 列为直接原因。州官员敦促居民检查他们的 疫苗接种 状态,并分发数千剂 MMR 疫苗 疫苗以减缓传播。麻疹 已导致 9 人因严重症状住院,强调了 麻疹 对幼儿和免疫功能低下个体的风险。DHS 建议高风险地区 6 至 11 个月大的婴儿尽早接受 疫苗接种,并正在努力增强社区免疫力。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该婴儿的死亡报告于 2026 年 11 月 6 日报道,截至该日期,暴发 已涵盖西南四个县的近 400 例病例。
地点与对象
该事件发生在威斯康星州西南部,特别是在格兰特县、爱荷华州、拉斐特县和弗农县,影响了婴儿和未接种疫苗的个人。
事件
威斯康星州卫生官员证实了一名与 麻疹 麻疹 有关的婴儿死亡,这是该州 35 年来首例与 麻疹 相关的死亡事件。威斯康星州卫生服务部 (DHS) 报告了这起死亡事件,并表示 麻疹 被列为直接死亡原因。
规模、影响与风险
截至 2026 年 11 月 6 日,威斯康星州 13 个县共有 431 例 麻疹 病例,其中 99% 以上的病例与未接种疫苗的个人有关。九名威斯康星州人因严重症状需要住院治疗,暴发 被描述为低估了感染情况。婴儿的死亡凸显了 麻疹 对幼儿和免疫系统较弱的人的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
DHS 建议高风险地区 6 至 11 个月大的婴儿尽早接受 疫苗接种。他们还敦促居民检查他们的 疫苗接种 状态,并自 2026 年 8 月以来分发了超过 42,000 剂 MMR 疫苗 剂量。该州正在努力提高 疫苗接种 率并防止进一步传播。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Infant’s death linked to measles outbreak in southwest Wisconsin

News Brief
An infant’s death has been linked to a measles outbreak in southwestern Wisconsin, marking the first measles-related fatality in the state in over 35 years. The outbreak, which has affected nearly 400 individuals across four counties, is attributed to low vaccination rates, with over 99% of cases involving unvaccinated people. The Wisconsin Department of Health Services (DHS) confirmed the death and listed measles as the immediate cause. State officials are urging residents to review their vaccination status and are distributing thousands of MMR vaccine doses to mitigate the spread. The outbreak has led to nine hospitalizations for severe symptoms, emphasizing the risks of measles for young children and immunocompromised individuals. DHS is recommending early vaccination for infants aged 6 to 11 months in high-risk areas and is working to enhance community immunity.
Original source text (English)
Skip to content Bsky Twitter TikTok YouTube Facebook Instagram Wisconsin Watch Nonprofit, nonpartisan news about Wisconsin Free newsletters Become a supporter Sign In Open Search Search for: Search Become a supporter Menu Sign In About us Open dropdown menu Our people Our Board of Directors Our ethics Our funding Our impact Support our work Open dropdown menu Ways to give or donate Join the Watchdog Club or Leadership Circle Join the Legacy Society Coverage areas Open dropdown menu State government Data Centers Watch Education & Employment Agriculture & Environment Health & Welfare Justice & Safety Community Opinion Major projects Open dropdown menu Forward Northeast News Fact Briefs Data tools Supreme costs Forgotten homes Prison Problems Q&Aging Latest stories Events Posted inHealth & Welfare Infant’s death linked to measles outbreak in southwest Wisconsin It’s the state’s first death connected to the ongoing outbreak that’s included nearly 400 cases across four southwestern counties by Hope Kirwan / WPR November 6, 2026November 6, 2026 Why you can trust Wisconsin Watch Share this: Share on Facebook (Opens in new window) Facebook Share on X (Opens in new window) X Share on Bluesky (Opens in new window) Bluesky Share on Threads (Opens in new window) Threads Share on Reddit (Opens in new window) Reddit Add Wisconsin Watch to Google A colorized transmission electron micrograph of a measles virus particle (yellow and red) is shown. Microscopy is courtesy of the U.S. Centers for Disease Control; layout, colorization and visual effects by National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases. Reading Time: 3 minutes Wisconsin health officials reported the death of an infant related to the ongoing measles outbreak in southwestern Wisconsin. It’s the first measles-associated death in the state in more than 35 years, according to the Wisconsin Department of Health Services. DHS reported that the coroner listed measles as the immediate cause of death. State officials said no further information about the death is being released to protect the privacy of the child and the family. DHS Secretary Kirsten Johnson said in a statement that her “heart is broken for the family.” “Any death of a child is a tragedy and even more so when it is related to a vaccine-preventable disease,” Johnson said in the statement. “Measles can cause serious illness and death, and it is important for all Wisconsinites to evaluate their risk and take steps to protect themselves and their family.” Doctors recommend children get their first dose of the vaccine for measles, mumps and rubella, or MMR, between 12 and 15 months. But Dr. Ryan Westergaard, DHS chief medical officer and state epidemiologist for communicable diseases, announced Thursday on a call with reporters that the state is now recommending infants who are 6 months to 11 months old receive an early dose of the shot if they are at an increased risk of exposure due to the outbreak. “In an outbreak, when we know that an infant may be exposed to measles, there is a benefit to providing that protection earlier,” Westergaard said. “For families with babies younger than 6 months or others who cannot be vaccinated, the most important protection is making sure that the people around them are immune. That’s one of the reasons that maintaining high vaccination coverage is so important.” Kids younger than 5 years old are among those most at risk of serious complications from measles, along with people who are pregnant and those with weakened immune systems. The highly contagious virus can cause pneumonia and swelling of the brain. Westergaard said the red, bumpy rash associated with measles doesn’t usually occur until several days after someone becomes sick. He told reporters than an infection typically starts with fever, cough, runny nose, and red eyes, and he urged people to avoid contact with others as soon as these symptoms occur. “We need people to recognize measles infections when they do occur and do everything we can to avoid exposing others,” said Westergaard, adding that those with symptoms should contact their local health department or medical provider for further guidance. Related Stories Low vaccination rates drive southwest Wisconsin measles outbreak Measles is in Wisconsin. Are Milwaukee schools vulnerable? ‘I would never wish it on anyone:’ Measles resurgence spurs memories of past toll in Wisconsin Measles has made it to Wisconsin. Here’s what to know about the virus. There have been 431 measles cases across 13 Wisconsin counties as of Tuesday. It’s the highest number of cases in the state in more than 35 years, according to DHS. And it’s “almost certainly an underestimate” of infections, according to Westergaard. He told reporters that not everyone who becomes infected gets tested or comes to the attention of public health. Westergaard pointed out that widespread vaccination changed measles from a common childhood infection to a relatively rare disease until recent years. “That was an extraordinary public health achievement, but it wasn’t permanent and it wasn’t inevitable that we would have that success,” he said. “It depended on doing the hard work of maintaining high levels of immunity in our communities. The death we’re reporting today is a tragic reminder of what can happen when measles begins circulating again.” The vast majority of cases in Wisconsin this year have been linked to spread of the virus among residents of Grant, Iowa, Lafayette and Vernon counties. The remaining measles cases have been linked to people being infected with measles during travel out of state. So far, nine Wisconsinites have required hospitalization for severe symptoms from measles. Over 99% of cases have been in people who are unvaccinated. According to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, there have been four other measles-related deaths reported in the U.S. this year. The MMR vaccine is 97% effective at preventing measles when the person has completed the medically recommended two-dose course. State health officials are urging residents to check their and their families’ vaccination status through the state’s online registry or with their medical provider. DHS reported some progress on improving the state’s vaccination rates. Since Aug. 1, the state has delivered 2,000 doses of the MMR vaccine to local health departments in measles-affected communities. Across the state, over 42,000 doses of the vaccine have been administered since Aug. 1. This story was originally published by WPR. 加拿大的 麻疹 暴发 集中在疫苗接种不足的人群中 - Medscape

Canada’s Measles Outbreak Concentrated Among Undervaccinated - Medscape
新闻简报2026 年 11 月,Medscape 报道了加拿大的 麻疹 暴发 病例,该病例集中在疫苗接种不足的社区,强调了即使在以前控制了该疾病的国家也存在复发的风险。暴发 于 2024 年 10 月开始,于 10 月 19 日宣布结束,在 49 周内涉及 2377 例病例(2064 例确诊,313 例疑似),大多数病例发生在 19 岁或以下的个人和未接种疫苗的人群中。安大略省公共卫生部门的莎拉·威尔逊医学博士指出,麻疹 的高覆盖率提供了人口层面的保护,但社区内持续存在的免疫缺口可能导致 麻疹 长时间传播。麻疹 与 166 例住院、13 例入住 ICU、51 例妊娠期间感染、10 例先天性 麻疹 感染、1 例脑炎和 1 例婴儿死亡相关。威尔逊强调了持续、文化上适当的参与和灵活的 疫苗接种 战略对于解决免疫差距的重要性。麻疹 还表明需要对 麻疹 保持警惕,因为它仍然是一种高度传播的病毒。研究人员利用系统发育分析来识别一个新序列,并记录其国际传播情况。研究结果强调了公共卫生成就的脆弱性以及维持高 疫苗 覆盖率以防止未来 暴发 的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发于2024年10月启动,10月19日正式宣告结束,新闻报道发布日期为2026年11月6日。
地点与对象
暴发 发生在加拿大,特别是在安大略省疫苗接种不足的社区。大多数病例 (90.3%) 是已建立的疫苗接种不足社区的成员,只有有限的 跨物种溢出 进入更广泛的人群。大约 73% 的病例发生在 19 岁或以下的个体中,89.2% 的感染患者未接种疫苗。
事件
加拿大报告了 麻疹病毒 的 暴发,集中在疫苗接种不足的社区。研究人员分析了 麻疹 期间向安大略省公共卫生 监测 系统报告的 麻疹 病例,证实了这一事件。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 包括 49 周内的 2377 例病例(2064 例确诊病例,313 例疑似病例)。它与 166 例住院、13 例入住 ICU、51 例妊娠期间感染、10 例先天性 麻疹 感染、1 例脑炎和 1 例婴儿死亡有关。麻疹 证明了 麻疹 在先前已控制疾病的国家中复发的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
使用 暴发 特定定义结合流行病学、临床、实验室和病毒测序标准对 暴发 进行分析。安大略省公共卫生部门和研究人员确定了 暴发 及其影响,强调需要持续的、文化上适当的参与和灵活的 疫苗接种 方法。麻疹于10月19日宣布结束,加拿大的麻疹消除状态只有在当前菌株至少连续12个月停止后才能恢复。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Canada’s Measles Outbreak Concentrated Among Undervaccinated - Medscape

News Brief
In November 2026, Medscape reported on a measles outbreak in Canada that was concentrated in undervaccinated communities, highlighting the risk of resurgence even in countries that had previously controlled the disease. The outbreak, which began in October 2024 and was declared over in October 19, involved 2377 cases (2064 confirmed and 313 probable) over 49 weeks, with most cases occurring among individuals aged 19 or younger and unvaccinated people. Public Health Ontario’s Sarah Wilson, MD, noted that high vaccination coverage provides population-level protection, but persistent immunity gaps within communities can allow measles to spread for prolonged periods. The outbreak was associated with 166 hospital admissions, 13 ICU admissions, 51 infections during pregnancy, 10 congenital measles infections, one case of encephalitis, and one infant death. Wilson emphasized the importance of sustained, culturally appropriate engagement and flexible vaccination strategies to address immunity gaps. The outbreak also demonstrated the need for vigilance against measles, as it remains a highly transmissible virus. Researchers used phylogenetic analysis to identify a novel sequence and noted its international spread. The findings underscore the fragility of public health achievements and the necessity of maintaining high vaccine coverage to prevent future outbreaks.
Original source text (English)
Your Clinical Hub News & Perspective Tools & Reference CME/CE More EN RegisterLog In Ask AI Search AI Mode Medscape News Canada Canada’s Measles Outbreak Concentrated Among Undervaccinated Marilynn Larkin November 06, 2026 2 Add to Email Alerts The outbreak that contributed to Canada’s loss of its measles elimination status was concentrated in undervaccinated communities, highlighting the risk for measles resurgence even in countries that previously controlled the disease, researchers warn. The outbreak started in October 2024 and was officially declared over on October 19. But Canada’s measles elimination status, which was first achieved in 1998, can be regained only after the current strain is stopped for at least 12 consecutive months. Meanwhile, Canada has learned “several important public health lessons” from the study, Sarah Wilson, MD, deputy chief of medical and systems support at Public Health Ontario in Toronto, told Medscape News Canada. The analysis of measles cases reported to Ontario’s public health surveillance system during the outbreak found that high vaccination coverage provides important population-level protection, she said. “Despite the size of the outbreak, spread into the broader population was limited, demonstrating the protection associated with high vaccine acceptance.” Sarah Wilson, MD Nevertheless, “persistent immunity gaps can allow measles to spread rapidly,” said Wilson. “In Ontario, about 90% of outbreak cases occurred in historically undervaccinated communities, and 89% were among people who were unvaccinated. This [finding] reinforces the importance of identifying and closing immunity gaps before an imported case can lead to sustained transmission.” Overall, she said, the outbreak “highlighted the importance of sustained, culturally appropriate engagement and adapting the response to the needs of affected communities, including trusted communications, lower-barrier testing, and flexible vaccination approaches such as community-based clinics and home visits.” SUGGESTED FOR YOU The study was published online on October 28 in The BMJ. Public Health Achievements Fragile Researchers gathered demographic characteristics, vaccination status, hospital admissions, complications, pregnancy-associated outcomes, mortality, and evidence of international spread for the cohort of measles cases reported to Ontario’s public health surveillance system during the outbreak. Cases were identified using an outbreak-specific definition incorporating epidemiologic, clinical, laboratory, and viral sequencing criteria. Phylogenetic analysis of measles virus sequences was used to assess evidence of spread to other countries. The outbreak included 2377 cases (2064 confirmed and 313 probable) over 49 weeks. Most cases (90.3%) occurred among members of established undervaccinated communities, with limited spillover into the broader population. “The finding that stood out to us was just how concentrated the outbreak remained within communities with longstanding low immunization coverage, despite the very large number of cases and the extraordinary infectiousness of measles,” Wilson said. “In some ways, that is reassuring because it demonstrates the population-level protection provided by high vaccine coverage. At the same time, it shows how substantial immunity gaps within particular communities can allow measles to spread for a prolonged period, even in a country where overall vaccine acceptance remains relatively high.” Approximately 73% of cases occurred in individuals aged 19 years or younger; 89.2% of infected patients were unvaccinated, and 4.1% had unknown vaccination status. The outbreak was associated with 166 (7%) hospital admissions, 13 (< 1%) admissions to an ICU, 51 (2.1%) infections during pregnancy, 10 (< 1%) congenital measles infections, one (< 1%) case of encephalitis, and one (< 1%) infant death. The researchers were struck by the impact on pregnant individuals and young infants, Wilson said. “The outbreak highlighted that longstanding immunity gaps can persist into adulthood and have consequences beyond childhood disease.” In addition, the sequencing analysis identified a novel N450 distinct sequence identifier that was subsequently detected in other countries, including the US, Mexico, and Netherlands. In a related editorial, Jennifer B. Nuzzo, DrPH, director of the Pandemic Center at Brown University School of Public Health in Providence, Rhode Island, warned, “Canada’s experience underscores the fragility of hard-won public health achievements and the need for countries to recommit to elimination, not only to prevent needless illness and death but also to strengthen defenses against risk of infectious diseases more broadly.” Measles Not Consigned to the Past Regarding strategies to reverse low vaccine uptake and sustain culturally appropriate engagement, Wilson said, “There isn’t a single strategy that will work for every community. The reasons for longstanding low vaccine uptake are complex and can include structural barriers to accessing care, historical experiences with health systems, and community-specific perspectives on vaccination.” “What is important is sustained engagement rather than engagement that begins and ends when there is an outbreak,” she explained. “That means working with trusted community members and local healthcare providers to understand the specific barriers and concerns within a community, making vaccination convenient and accessible, and providing information in ways that are culturally and linguistically appropriate.” Clinicians can help by using routine healthcare encounters “as opportunities to review immunization status, identify people who may have missed doses, answer questions without judgment, and offer vaccination where appropriate,” she said. “Immunity gaps are not always immediately obvious. Someone may have missed vaccination many years ago and remain susceptible into adulthood. Clinicians can help close those gaps before someone is exposed to measles, rather than waiting until an outbreak occurs.” The practical message is simple, she said. “Don’t assume measles is a disease of the past. Maintaining high vaccine coverage, identifying people who remain susceptible, and considering measles when the clinical and epidemiologic picture fits remain important even in countries that have previously achieved elimination.” Brian Ward, MD Brian Ward, MD, professor of medicine at McGill University and senior scientist at the Research Institute of the McGill University Health Centre in Montreal, commented on the study for Medscape News Canada. “As long as large pockets of under- and unimmunized people exist and people continue to move around the world in airplanes, outbreaks like this will continue to occur,” he said. “Measles is one of the most transmissible human viruses (much more transmissible than influenza, for example), so even transient exposures can contribute to rapidly expanding chains of transmission in susceptible populations.” While Ward agreed that culturally appropriate engagement is needed, “This is far easier said than done,” he noted. “Trust takes time and effort to build in some communities. Identifying community ‘champions’ who can communicate in culturally sensitive ways and who are a priori trusted can be an effective approach.” Yet, he added, “These kinds of links with some communities can be difficult, if not impossible, to establish in the setting of an ongoing outbreak.” Clinicians can help by staying up to date on vaccine-preventable infections, taking every opportunity to review the vaccination status of individuals and promote vaccines, and “maintaining a high degree of suspicion for measles cases in susceptible populations so that appropriate resources can be mobilized as early as possible to keep the inevitable next outbreak as small as possible,” he said. “Given the level of misinformation about vaccines in general and about measles vaccination in particular, we have to be ready to take the time to offer a true narrative to hesitant parents,” he added. “Many antivaccine voices compare the dangers of vaccination to no illness, which has never been a fair comparison. The recent Canadian and ongoing outbreaks in the US and other countries make it clear that any concerns about ‘risks’ of measles vaccination (real or imagined) must be compared to the real risks of getting measles.” “Although we tend to focus on the immediate effects of measles like hospitalizations, most people (including clinicians) don’t know that measles effectively targets our immune system and can have profound and long-term negative effects on immunity.” The study was not supported by outside funding. Wilson, Nuzzo, and Ward reported having no relevant financial relationships. Marilynn Larkin, MA, is an award-winning medical writer and editor based in New York City whose work has appeared in numerous publications, including Medscape Medical News and its sister publication MDedge, The Lancet (where she was a contributing editor), and Reuters Health. 研究:麻疹 可能在康复数年后导致致命性脑部疾病

Measles Can Cause Fatal Brain Disease Years After Recovery: Study
新闻简报发表在《新发传染病》上的一项研究表明,麻疹 在康复数年后可能会导致一种罕见但几乎总是致命的神经系统疾病,称为 亚急性硬化性全脑炎 (SSPE)。这项研究在菲律宾进行,分析了 2007 年至 2022 年期间入住国家儿童医院的 20 名儿童,其中 2020 年至 2022 年期间收治了 8 名儿童,仅 2022 年就有 5 名儿童入院。其中 4 例与 2018-2019 年 麻疹 暴发 有关,估计感染发生在 2.1-3.5 年前。SSPE 通常在 麻疹 感染后几年发生,特别是在儿童早期,是一种进行性神经退行性疾病。该研究强调了通过 麻疹 预防 麻疹 的重要性,并建议对神经功能恶化的儿童进行脑脊液 麻疹 抗体检测。它还强调需要改进 SSPE 监测 和维持两剂 疫苗接种 覆盖范围。该研究受到样本量小、单中心焦点以及依赖父母回忆 疫苗接种 历史的限制。研究结果强调了 麻疹 感染的长期风险以及 麻疹 在预防此类并发症中的关键作用。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究发表于 2026-11-05T20:00:41+05:30,并于 2026 年 11 月 6 日更新。活动日期范围为 2007 年 1 月至 2022 年 12 月,最近入院人数为 2020 年至 2022 年。
地点与对象
该研究在菲律宾进行,重点关注 2007 年 1 月至 2022 年 12 月期间入住国家儿童医院的儿童。受影响人群包括 亚急性硬化性全脑炎 (SSPE) 儿童。
事件
发表在《新发传染病》上的一项研究发现,麻疹 在康复数年后可能会导致一种罕见但几乎总是致命的神经系统疾病,称为 亚急性硬化性全脑炎 (SSPE)。这项研究是由奎松市国家儿童医院和菲律宾马尼拉圣贝达大学医学院的研究人员进行的。
规模、影响与风险
该研究回顾了 20 名被诊断患有 SSPE 的儿童,其中 8 名儿童在 2020 年至 2022 年期间入院,仅 2022 年就有 5 名儿童入院。其中 4 例与 2018-2019 年 麻疹 暴发 有关,估计感染发生在 2.1-3.5 年前。SSPE 是一种进行性神经退行性疾病,几乎总是致命的,通常在 麻疹 感染几年后发生,特别是在儿童早期。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究强调了通过 麻疹 预防 麻疹 的重要性,并建议对进行性神经功能恶化的儿童进行脑脊液 麻疹 抗体检测。它还强调需要加强 SSPE 监测 和维持两剂 麻疹 疫苗接种 覆盖范围。该研究受到其小、单中心样本、缺乏人群水平数据以及依赖父母或监护人回忆 疫苗接种 历史的限制。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles Can Cause Fatal Brain Disease Years After Recovery: Study

News Brief
A study published in Emerging Infectious Diseases reveals that measles can lead to a rare but almost always fatal neurological condition called subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE) years after recovery. Conducted in the Philippines, the research analyzed 20 children admitted to the National Children’s Hospital between 2007 and 2022, with 8 admitted during 2020–2022 and 5 in 2022 alone. Four of these cases were linked to the 2018–2019 measles outbreak, with infections estimated to have occurred 2.1–3.5 years prior. SSPE typically develops several years after measles infection, especially in early childhood, and is a progressive neurodegenerative disorder. The study underscores the importance of measles prevention through vaccination and recommends cerebrospinal fluid measles antibody testing for children with neurological deterioration. It also highlights the need for improved SSPE surveillance and maintaining two-dose vaccination coverage. The research was limited by its small sample size, single-center focus, and reliance on parental recall for vaccination histories. The findings emphasize the long-term risks of measles infection and the critical role of vaccination in preventing such complications.
Original source text (English)

Measles Can Cause Fatal Brain Disease Years After Recovery: Study Written By : Medha Baranwal ,Medically Reviewed By : Dr. Kamal Kant Kohli Published On 2026-11-05T20:00:41+05:30 | Updated On 6 Nov 2026 12:23 PM IST Philippines: Measles is not merely a rash-producing illness; it can cause serious complications even years after apparent recovery. A rare but almost invariably fatal neurological condition, subacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE), can develop years after measles infection. A new report published in Emerging Infectious Diseases highlights this delayed complication, highlighting the importance of measles prevention through vaccination. The study, led by Maela P. Palisoc of the National Children’s Hospital, Quezon City, and the College of Medicine, San Beda University, Manila, Philippines, and colleagues, reviewed children diagnosed with SSPE over 16 years. The researchers warned that additional cases linked to the country's 2018–2019 measles outbreak could emerge in the coming years because of the long interval between infection and neurological symptoms. SSPE is a progressive neurodegenerative disorder caused by persistent infection of the central nervous system with a defective measles virus. It typically develops several years after measles infection, particularly when infection occurs during early childhood, and is almost always fatal. For the study, researchers retrospectively examined medical records of 20 children admitted with SSPE to the National Children’s Hospital between January 2007 and December 2022. They assessed clinical characteristics, vaccination history, reported measles infection, and the estimated timing of infection in relation to national measles outbreaks. The study findings revealed the following: Recent increase in admissions: Eight of the 20 children (40%) were admitted during 2020–2022, while five (25%) were admitted in 2022 alone. Cases linked to the latest outbreak: Four children had estimated measles infection dates corresponding to the 2018–2019 outbreak. All were admitted between 2020 and 2022, approximately 2.1–3.5 years after infection. Early childhood infection: The median age at reported measles illness was 0.7 years, while the median age at hospital admission was 6.2 years. Ten of the 19 children with an estimable age at infection had contracted measles before nine months of age. Incomplete measles history: A history of measles was reported in 15 children (75%), was unknown in four (20%), and was absent in one (5%). Vaccination history: Seventeen children (85%) had reportedly received at least one measles-containing vaccine, although 11 (55%) had received only one dose. Vaccination histories were based on parental or guardian recall and were not consistently verified against records. Declining measles vaccine coverage in the Philippines may have increased susceptibility to infection, with first-dose coverage falling to 64% in 2021 amid repeated outbreaks. The researchers recommended cerebrospinal fluid measles antibody testing in children with progressive neurological deterioration and myoclonus, even without a reported history of measles, to avoid missing SSPE diagnoses. The study was limited by its small, single-center sample, lack of population-level data, and reliance on recalled histories. The researchers emphasized strengthening SSPE surveillance and sustaining two-dose measles vaccination coverage to prevent future cases. Reference: Palisoc MP, Ramones MS, Jamora RDG. Subacute sclerosing panencephalitis after measles outbreaks, the Philippines, 2007–2022. Emerg Infect Dis. 2026 Nov [date cited]. https://doi.org/10.3201/eid3211.261395 Emerging Infectious DiseasesSubacute sclerosing panencephalitis (SSPE)Measles Outbreaks Source : Emerging Infectious Diseases Medical Survey Medha Baranwal MSc. B routine priorityPublic-health news

PA measles cases top 1,400; Berks still at 7 in latest update - Reading Eagle

News Brief
As of November 3, 2026, Pennsylvania has reported 1,433 confirmed measles cases across 44 counties, with Berks County at 7 cases and Lancaster County leading with 496. The state health department has administered over 6,800 measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) vaccinations since the outbreak began in late April, with more than 10,600 doses given this year. Five measles-associated deaths have been recorded, and 269 hospitalizations have occurred. Measles spreads through respiratory droplets and remains infectious in the air and on surfaces for up to two hours. The department recommends an additional early zero dose for infants between 6 and 11 months in high-risk areas, though this does not replace the two routine doses. Adults with only one dose or no records should consult healthcare providers for vaccination. The outbreak continues to spread, prompting ongoing public health responses.
Original source text (English)
An electron microscope image of a measles virus particle. (Cynthia S. Goldsmith; William Bellini, Ph.D./Centers for Disease Control and Prevention/TNS/TNS) By David Mowery | dmowery@readingeagle.com | Reading Eagle PUBLISHED: November 3, 2026 at 8:15 AM EST The statewide total of confirmed measles cases has reached 1,433 across 44 counties. Berks County continues to hold at seven cases in the update issued Monday by the sate Department of Health. The state is reporting five measles-associated deaths. The department’s update noted the following: • New positive cases since Oct. 30: 7 • Total counties: 44 • Total cumulative cases: 1,433 • Total hospitalizations: 269 • Total confirmed measles-associated deaths: 5 • Measles cases among vaccinated individuals: 4 (less than 1% of cases) According to the department’s dashboard at pa.gov/measles , Lancaster County remains at the top in cases with 496. Cases in the region: Chester County, 93; Lebanon County, 22; Schuylkill County, 7; Montgomery County, 3; and Lehigh County, 2. State health center staff have administered more than 6,800 measles, mumps and rubella (MMR) vaccinations at 238 pop-up vaccination clinics in affected communities since the outbreak began in late April, officials said. Overall, the department has given more than 10,600 MMR vaccine doses this year. Officials said healthcare providers continue to see an increase in residents interested in the MMR vaccinations. Providers typically administer about 25,000 doses of the vaccine a month. They said more than 56,000 doses were given in September and that more than 53,000 MMR vaccines were given in October. Because measles continues to spread, the department said it recommends an additional early zero dose for infants between the ages of 6 and 11 months who live in or travel to counties flagged on the measles dashboard for sustained community transmission. They said the early dose does not replace the two routine doses. Children 12 months and older who have not received any dose should get one now and a second dose at least 28 days later. Those who have two documented doses of MMR do not need another, the department said. Adults who have only one dose or cannot find their vaccination records should talk with a healthcare provider or pharmacist about getting vaccinated. Measles is spread when someone who is infected breathes, coughs or sneezes. The virus remains infectious in the air and on surfaces for up to two hours after the infected person leaves the area, the department said. Symptoms begin with fever, cough, runny nose, and red, watery eyes (conjunctivitis), followed by a rash that starts on the head and spreads downward. Without proper protection or supportive care, measles can be deadly, officials said. Symptoms can start seven to 21 days after exposure. If you believe you were exposed to measles and are experiencing symptoms, you are advised to contact your healthcare provider or call the health department’s hotline at 877-PA-HEALTH (877-724-3258). 纽约30年来首例麻疹死亡预示着更糟糕的事情即将来临

New York’s first measles death in 30 years is an omen of worse to come
新闻简报2026 年 10 月 26 日星期一,纽约州北部的一名儿童死于 麻疹,这是该州 30 年来首例 麻疹 死亡病例。该事件是美国自 1990 年代以来最大的 麻疹 麻疹 事件的一部分,截至 2026 年 10 月 29 日,CDC 报告了 4,852 例确诊病例。纽约州已出现 178 例病例,主要发生在中部地区,这起死亡事件突显了由于 麻疹 发病率下降而导致的 麻疹 风险不断增加。孩子的 疫苗接种 状况仍不清楚,暴发 引起了人们对先天性 麻疹 的担忧,如果孕妇感染该病毒,可能会影响未出生的孩子。州长 Kathy Hochul 宣布 麻疹 为全州范围的灾难,并准备与官员会面,评估全国无 麻疹 的状态,目前正在审查中。与 2025 年相比,麻疹 的病例数量增加了一倍,这是自 1991 年以来 麻疹 最糟糕的一年,专家警告可能存在长期健康影响。疾病预防控制中心报告了全国三例死亡病例,一些州对这些数字存在争议,情况仍在调查中。
查看新闻五要素
时间
纽约首例 麻疹 死亡发生在 2026 年 10 月 26 日星期一。截至 2026 年 10 月 29 日,CDC 确认了 4,852 例 麻疹 病例,截至 2026 年 10 月 30 日,全国有 3 例死亡。麻疹 自 2025 年以来一直在进行,病例数比前一年翻了一番。
地点与对象
The death occurred in Lewis County, New York, which is northeast of Syracuse. The outbreak is mainly concentrated in the central part of New York State. The affected population includes unvaccinated individuals and those with unknown vaccination status, with 96 percent of reported cases falling into these categories.
事件
纽约州北部的一名儿童在感染 麻疹 后死亡,这是该州 30 年来首例 麻疹 死亡病例。该事件由 CDC 报告,是美国三十多年来最大的 麻疹 暴发 的一部分。
规模、影响与风险
As of October 29, 2026, the CDC had confirmed 4,852 measles cases nationwide, with New York State reporting 178 cases. The death rate is 11 percent nationally, with three confirmed deaths. The outbreak has raised concerns about congenital measles, which can occur if a pregnant woman contracts the virus, and the potential for further spread due to declining vaccination rates.
应对、状态与不确定性
纽约州州长 Kathy Hochul 上个月宣布 麻疹 为全州范围的灾难,并将与官员会面,以确定是否应修改该国的 麻疹 无状态。疾控中心尚未证实死者儿童是否接种了疫苗,情况仍在调查中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

New York’s first measles death in 30 years is an omen of worse to come

News Brief
A child in upstate New York died from measles on Monday, October 26, 2026, marking the first measles death in the state in 30 years. This incident is part of the largest measles outbreak in the U.S. since the 1990s, with the CDC reporting 4,852 confirmed cases as of October 29, 2026. New York State has seen 178 cases, mainly in its central region, and the death highlights the growing risk of measles due to declining vaccination rates. The child’s vaccination status remains unclear, and the outbreak has raised concerns about congenital measles, which can affect unborn children if a pregnant woman contracts the virus. Governor Kathy Hochul declared measles a statewide disaster and is preparing to meet with officials to assess the national measles-free status, which is currently under review. The outbreak has doubled case numbers from 2025, the worst year for measles since 1991, and experts warn of potential long-term health impacts. The CDC has reported three deaths nationally, with some states disputing these figures, and the situation remains under investigation.
Original source text (English)
New York’s first measles death in 30 years is an omen of worse to come A child died after contracting the disease amid the largest national measles outbreak in more than three decades By Mary Randolph edited by Claire Cameron Join Our Community of Science Lovers! A child in upstate New York died on Monday after contracting measles . This marks the state’s first death from the disease in 30 years, as the outbreak continues to spread across the country. In a statement, New York governor Kathy Hochul said she was “heartbroken” and that the death reflected a crisis of government and public health. Hochul declared measles a statewide disaster last month. With fewer immune protections, children are especially at risk for measles and its more dangerous effects. And as the outbreak surges, doctors are becoming increasingly concerned about congenital measles. If a pregnant woman has measles, there’s a 25 to 30 percent chance her child will also acquire measles, according to the American Academy of Pediatrics. Congenital measles is not tracked by the Center for Disease Control and Prevention—the condition had become exceedingly rare, but rising rates are raising fears about its potential effects once more. If you're enjoying this article, consider supporting our award-winning journalism by subscribing . By purchasing a subscription you are helping to ensure the future of impactful stories about the discoveries and ideas shaping our world today. As of October 29, the CDC had confirmed 4,852 measles cases across the U.S. so far this year. New York State has had a total of 178 confirmed cases, mainly concentrated in the central part of the state. According to officials, the recent death in New York occurred in Lewis County, which is northeast of Syracuse and has seen 16 cases in 2026. It is representative of a worsening national picture. The U.S. is currently in the thick of the worst measles outbreak since 1991. Case numbers this year have already doubled those of 2025 —previously the worst year for the disease since 1991. (The U.S. was declared measles-free in 2000.) Technically, the country still holds its measles-free status , a title that is marked by 12 months without continuous local transmission, although experts expect that may change soon. Officials are set to meet in the next few weeks to determine whether that status—hailed for years as a symbol of excellence in public health—should be revised or eliminated. As of October 30, the CDC has reported three deaths from measles nationally—although some states have reported deaths that have been disputed by the CDC—and an 11 percent hospitalization rate. In its milder forms, the disease can cause an itchy rash and fever, but it can also cause pneumonia and severe brain inflammation, which, without treatment, can prove fatal or disabling. The measles virus is the most contagious virus known—but it is not impossible to contain. Vaccines, particularly the measles, mumps and rubella (MMR) combined shot, had helped measles fade because of herd immunity. But dwindling vaccination rates are helping the disease break through, with dire consequences. The MMR jab offers 97 percent protection for life after two doses given in childhood and was the major factor in eradicating measles in the U.S. But with higher levels of vaccine skepticism among the public and the upper echelons of the Trump administration, fewer children are receiving the vaccine: in the past 10 years, MMR vaccination rates among kindergarteners have fallen from 94 percent to 92.4 percent. (A 95 percent vaccination rate is the critical number to ensure community or herd immunity.) Of the cases reported by the CDC this year, 96 percent were among people who were unvaccinated or whose vaccination status was unknown. It’s unclear whether the child that died on Monday was vaccinated. Editor’s Note (11/3/26): This is a breaking news story and may be updated. A child in upstate New York died after contracting measles, marking the state’s first measles death in 30 years. This event is reported by the CDC and is part of the largest measles outbreak in the U.S. in over three decades.

As of October 29, 2026, the CDC had confirmed 4,852 measles cases nationwide, with New York State reporting 178 cases. The death rate is 11 percent nationally, with three confirmed deaths. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Joint Statement From New York State Department of Health and Lewis County Public Health on Pediatric Measles-Associated Death in Lewis County - the wellsville sun

News Brief
A joint statement from the New York State Department of Health and Lewis County Public Health confirmed a pediatric measles-associated death in Lewis County, marking the first such case in New York State in over 30 years. The outbreak has led to a State Disaster Emergency declaration, with 178 total cases reported as of October 24, 2026, including 170 outside New York City and 8 in the city. The measles virus, highly contagious and causing serious complications like pneumonia, brain swelling, and death, spreads through coughing, sneezing, or contact with contaminated surfaces. The Department is providing the MMR vaccine to unvaccinated individuals and urging healthcare providers to report suspected cases. Public health measures, including contact tracing and quarantine, are being implemented to control the outbreak. The statement emphasizes the importance of vaccination, particularly for infants 6 months and older, and recommends checking immunization status to prevent further infections.

Menu Menu Checkout Contact Us Dining Guide Listings My Account New User Registration Pollock: It’s not hyperbole, Saint Joe’s looms as Bonnies biggest game of the season Sun Support Home Obituaries Contact Us Dining Guide News, Politics, and Culture from 14895 November, by Craig Braack MENU Checkout Contact Us Dining Guide Listings My Account New User Registration Pollock: It’s not hyperbole, Saint Joe’s looms as Bonnies biggest game of the season Sun Support Search for: Home » Joint Statement From New York State Department of Health and Lewis County Public Health on Pediatric Measles-Associated Death in Lewis County November 2, 2026 Joint Statement From New York State Department of Health and Lewis County Public Health on Pediatric Measles-Associated Death in Lewis County Share: Tragedy marks 30 years since the last measles related death in NY From the NYS Department of Health ALBANY, N.Y. (November 2, 2026) – Based on information currently available, the New York State Department of Health and Lewis County Public Health issued the following joint statement on the first known measles-associated death in New York State in more than 30 years: “We are deeply saddened by the death of a child in Lewis County associated with the ongoing measles outbreak. We extend our heartfelt condolences to the child’s family and loved ones and ask that their privacy be respected during this difficult time. “Measles is a highly contagious disease that can cause serious complications and death. Vaccination is the best protection against measles. “The Department has offered resources to Lewis County to assist with community outreach and support local vaccination efforts – both in response to this case, and as part of our ongoing efforts to support counties that are facing this ongoing measles outbreak. “We urge New Yorkers to ensure they are up to date on measles, mumps and rubella (MMR) vaccinations and speak to their healthcare provider about immunizations.” Measles Prevention and Vaccine Information The news comes four weeks after Governor Hochul declared a State Disaster Emergency in response to the measles outbreak across areas of New York and neighboring states, expanding the pool of eligible individuals who can administer measles vaccines and providing swift access to additional resources if needed. The Department is working with county health departments and local partners to provide the measles, mumps and rubella vaccine to any unvaccinated individuals who request it and to closely monitor and investigate all reported cases. Monitoring and investigation activities, such as contact tracing, are ways public health officials work to keep affected communities safe from the spread of infectious diseases. Anyone who is contacted during these activities is encouraged to be responsive so that important information can be shared to prevent more infections. As of October 24, there have been 178 total cases this year: 170 in New York State outside of the city, and eight in New York City. Apply in Person or email Shorts1280@yahoo.com All New Yorkers are urged to protect themselves by making sure they are up to date on their measles, mumps, and rubella (MMR) vaccines and be aware of health advisories if traveling with unvaccinated individuals, especially young children. The latest guidance issued during this outbreak recommends that infants 6 months and older receive an early dose of the MMR vaccine for protection before travel or if they are otherwise at risk of being exposed, such as residence in a community with measles transmission. Measles is a highly contagious, serious respiratory disease that causes rash and fever. In some cases, measles can reduce the immune system’s ability to fight other infections like pneumonia. This disease is caused by a virus that is spread by coughing or sneezing into the air. Individuals can catch the disease by breathing in the virus or, less commonly, by touching a contaminated surface, then touching the eyes, nose, or mouth. Serious complications of measles include hospitalization, pneumonia, miscarriage, preterm birth, brain swelling, immune amnesia and death. Long-term serious complications can also include subacute sclerosing panencephalitis, a type of brain inflammation resulting from an earlier measles infection that can lead to permanent brain damage years after the initial infection. The best protection against measles is the measles, mumps and rubella (MMR) vaccine. Those traveling domestically or internationally should plan to be fully vaccinated against measles at least two weeks before departure. One dose provides 93 percent protection while two doses provide 97 percent protection. Babies as young as 6 months should get one dose of the MMR vaccine if they are traveling abroad or to an area where measles is prevalent or if they reside in a community with measles transmission. These babies should then continue to get their next two MMR vaccines on schedule for a total of three vaccines. Anyone who is not protected can get measles if exposed and can easily spread it to others when they return home. The Department has released a health advisory for healthcare providers on clinical guidance for measles. A person with measles can pass it to others as soon as four days before a rash appears and as late as four days after the rash appears. The incubation period for measles is up to 21 days. People who are exposed to measles and not immune should quarantine for 21 days after exposure, and those who test positive should isolate until four days after the rash appears. Symptoms of measles can include the following: Seven to 14 days, and up to 21 days after a measles infection High fever Cough Runny nose Red, watery eyes A rash can occur 3-5 days after symptoms become apparent: The rash usually begins as flat red spots that appear on the face at the hairline and spread downward to the neck, trunk, arms, legs and feet. Small, raised bumps may also appear on top of the flat red spots. The spots may become joined together as they spread from the head to the rest of the body. When the rash appears, a person’s fever may spike to more than 104° Fahrenheit. How Individuals Can Protect Themselves Individuals should check their vaccination status. Anyone who is unsure of their vaccination status should contact a healthcare provider or local health department. Know the symptoms. Measles often begins with fever, cough, runny nose, and red, watery eyes. A rash typically appears several days later, starting on the face and spreading across the body. Those who are sick should call ahead before visiting a clinic, urgent care, or emergency department so infection-control steps can be taken. Immunization Guidance The MMR vaccine is highly effective and the best protection against measles. Most people born before 1957 are considered immune because they were likely exposed to the virus. Those who received the vaccine between 1963 and 1967 may have received a vaccine that wasn’t as effective and should talk to their health care provider about getting revaccinated. Find the Department’s travel advisory urging New Yorkers to ensure they are fully vaccinated against measles before international travel or travel to an area in the U.S. experiencing an outbreak. Healthcare providers should report suspected measles cases to their local health department. Visit the Department’s dedicated measles website for information about measles, current case numbers, immunization data and information for providers. More information about the measles vaccine can be found here. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Wisconsin reports measles death of a baby, first death in over 35 years

News Brief
Wisconsin officials reported the death of a baby from measles, marking the first such death in the state since 1990. This incident is part of an ongoing measles outbreak in Wisconsin, which has affected over 430 people across 13 counties. The baby's death is the third measles fatality nationally this week, following similar cases in New York and Pennsylvania. The CDC is tracking 5,182 measles cases in the US this year, the highest since 1991, though underreporting is likely due to limited medical care access in affected communities. The US declared measles eliminated in 2000, but recent outbreaks indicate a potential resurgence. Officials emphasize the importance of vaccination to prevent such preventable tragedies.
